ParlaMint-PL_2016-08-03-senat-24-1.u6	High Izbo! Act on special rules of VAT settlement and implementation of... [[Talking in the O.R.]] [[Excuse me, Mr. Senator. Ladies and gentlemen of the Senators, we are now in session. Quiet, please. If anyone wants to talk, it's out of the O.R. You're welcome, Senator.]] The Act on the special rules for the settlement of VAT and the reimbursement of public funds for the implementation of projects financed by the budget of the European Union or by the Member States of the European Free Trade Agreement by local government units was adopted by the Sejm on 22 July this year. Yesterday, the joint committees, the Committee on Budget and Public Finance and the Committee on Territorial Government and State Administration examined this draft law. The Act introduces the principle that a local government unit will be required to account for VAT together with budgetary units and budgetary establishments. To date, the municipality was a separate taxable person, the budgetary unit was also a taxable person and the budgetary establishment was a taxable person. Where did this come from? This was due to the fact that on 29 September 2015 the judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union in the case of the municipality of Wrocław v Ministry of Finance concerning the interpretation of the article was handed down. 9 of the 2006 Directive, the Court of Justice of the European Union considered that the budgetary unit could not be a taxable person on its own, since this interpretation of the provisions of the Directive shows that it does not comply with the so-called. the independence criterion. The Court of the European Union found that the budgetary unit bears no risk of operating and takes up that activity on behalf of the municipality. Therefore, the municipality should be the taxable person for VAT. The result of that judgment was also the judgment of the Supreme Administrative Court, which stated that the same principle should apply to budgetary establishments. Therefore, it should be considered that local government units will be taxable persons for VAT, whereas they will not be budgetary establishments or budgetary units. It was therefore necessary to centralise VAT settlements for local authorities. This applies, of course, to local government, district government and regional government. This law gives self-government units legal tools in this area. It should be said that this is a law that has a special meaning. It is its special importance, its special status stems from the need to implement the judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union. The Legislative Office has proposed 15 amendments. 14 amendments were adopted as a result of the arrangements with the Ministry of Finance. As I say, these were amendments agreed between the Ministry of Finance and the Legislative Office. Unanimously, unanimously, the joint committees have accepted these 14 amendments and are asking the High House to adopt the law together with the amendments resulting from these amendments. For my part, I can add that in the Seym too, this law did not raise any controversy, as perhaps one of the few Members was not. against or abstained from voting. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-08-03-senat-24-1.u12	Thank you very much, Mr. Marshal. High Izbo! I would like to thank Senator Krzysztof Mroza for his thorough discussion of the work on the bill in the Senate committee. Allow me, however, to develop the subject, so that in this rather difficult, complex matter, which concerns the settlement of VAT in local governments, the state also gained such an extended, comprehensive knowledge, maybe also to explain... I know that senators are in contact with the leaders of local governments, local self-government units in municipalities, cities, powiats and voivodships, so that knowledge can take place. Mr Marshal, High Izbo, the main objective of the contested law is to provide local government units with legal instruments supporting the centralisation process and to reduce the burden of centralising VAT settlement in local governments in connection with the judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union of 29 September 2015 in Case C-276/14, Municipality of Wrocław... According to the Court of Justice, the municipal budgetary units do not bear the economic risks associated with their business activities on behalf and on behalf of the municipality and do not account for the damage caused by these activities because they do not have their own assets, do not earn their own income and do not bear the costs relating to such activities. The revenue generated is paid to the budget of the municipality and the expenditure is paid directly from that budget. The consequence of the VAT ruling is the absence of a tax separation of local budget units, which means that all their activities in favour of third parties should be accounted for by the local government unit in which the said budgetary units are made up. The judgment of the Court was not related to local budget facilities, but the Supreme Administrative Court adopted a resolution on 26 October last year in which it adopted, in relation to local budget facilities, an interpretation similar to that adopted by the Court of Justice in respect of local budgetary units. Consequently, the resolution adopted by the court should be considered to have no fiscal separation between local government and local government units under the VAT rules, so that all activities carried out by them in favour of third parties should be accounted for by the local government unit of which they are made up and the activities carried out within the local government, both between budgetary units and budgetary establishments, are internal. In view of the Court's judgment and the NSA's resolution, it is necessary to centralise VAT settlements in local governments as soon as possible. This centralisation must cover the organisational units set up by the local government, which are budgetary units, budgetary establishments and municipal offices, old offices and marshal offices. The concept adopted by the Act assumes mandatory centralisation of the settlements forward, i.e. from 1 January 2017, so that the judgment of the Court can be enforced with sufficient time for the local authorities to receive the accounts. The Minister of Finance had already informed the local authorities about this deadline in its communication, because the deadline was extended at the request of local governments. Initially, compulsory centralisation was assumed as early as 1 July of this year. The purpose of the support of local authorities in the centralisation process is to ensure that the funds allocated to the implementation of projects are not reimbursed in so far as a change in the eligibility of the tax has occurred, in the part relating to the amount of input tax financed by the funds allocated to the implementation of the projects. This law also provides for the absence of the need to centralise past accounts made prior to the Court's decision. If the local government wishes to exercise its right to deduct input tax, it will be able to make adjustments to the VAT return, starting from the earliest period for which the adjustment will be made. The government will decide what to do. Until centralisation, taking into account limitation periods. However, the condition for taking advantage of the deduction is In accordance with the judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union This means that complex corrections take account of the accounts of all business units and cover all settlement periods not covered by the statute in which the local government accounted for itself as separate from its business units, from the earliest accounting period for which the correction was submitted. Mr. Marshal, High Izbo, the regulations adopted by the Sejm are expected by local authorities, as they will guarantee the units of local government and their organisational units, which until the Court's judgment was delivered were treated on the basis of the VAT rules as separate taxpayers of this tax, the security of VAT settlement. To this end, the Act provides for the succession of rights and obligations to ensure entry On the date of the start of the joint settlement by the local government unit with its organisational units . in the rights and obligations of those entities, including with regard to past accounts. This means among others. there is no need to issue corrective invoices, as the local government unit will also acquire the right to deduct from invoices issued to its organisational units by entering into the rights and obligations of its units. The Act also provides for the possibility for organisational units to use, until the date of joint clearance, so-called. an individual exemption where, on the date of the judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union, those entities benefited from such an exemption. This will also apply to organisational units whose settlements were centralised with the local government unit prior to the entry into force of the Act. The centralisation process also requires that the question of the registration by the local government unit of sales to natural persons not engaged in business by means of registering offices be resolved. The centralisation of settlements without amending the relevant tax succession rules and registering banks would imply the need to exchange all previously used by the business units of the local government of fiscal cash registers and the purchase of new ones intended for entities which have so far benefited from the exemption from trading records. In order to minimise the financial costs that local authorities would have to bear in this area, it is envisaged that they may continue to record the turnover by means of cash registers whose fiscalisation has been carried out by the organisational units, but no longer than 31 December 2018. This regulation will exempt local authorities from the obligation to exchange cash registers or their fiscal memory and will allow the recording of turnover by means of cash registers already held. At the same time, the Act maintains until 31 December 2016 the exemption of local self-government units from the obligation to keep records of turnover and amounts of tax due by means of registering banks in respect of the activities of those entities which benefited from such exemption on the day preceding the date of centralisation. The absence of such provisions would imply the need to purchase fiscal cash registers for all organisational units which have benefited from the exemption so far. Given that the centralisation of accounts may give rise to the need for a large number of registering banks to be purchased by the local government after 31 December 2016, it is justified to extend exceptionally the participation of the State budget in the cost of purchasing those accounts, guaranteed in the current legislation only for the cashiers purchased at the time of the start of the registration obligation. Draft law from the very beginning, i.e. since the end of last year, has been constantly consulted At every stage of the legislative process With a local government environment, in particular within the framework of a specially established local working group. Mr Marshal, High Izbo, this law also introduces adjustments in the Corporate Income Tax Act and in the Public Finance Act, which will allow the transfer of amounts to a budget establishment when the local government unit receives a refund of the excess tax over due, resulting from the tax return. I ask that this law be adopted together with the amendments that have been discussed by the rapporteur.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-08-03-senat-24-1.u31	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to present the Government's position on the amendments to the Act of 22 July 2016 amending the Personal Income Tax Act and the Corporate Income Tax Act, Senate Printing No 255. Thank you very much to the rapporteur, who has brought these changes closer. Postulate, one of the basic policy demands and promises of Law and Justice, now Prime Minister Beata Szydło, to reduce tax on small taxpayers from 19% to 15% It is being implemented in the form of a law that we are discussing. The Act reduces the income tax rate for small CIT taxpayers by 4 percentage points, i.e. taxable persons whose sales income does not exceed EUR 1.2 million. As regards other information that should be submitted to the High House, I would like to draw attention to a fact that may be surprising: why the effects of this law, calculated in the impact assessment of the regulation, are indeed positive on the revenue side of the state budget and local government units. The Act also contains regulations on the sealing of the tax system and it is worth paying attention to the scope of these regulations. It is quite broad, sometimes overlooked, but it results in the fact that we can say in the assessment of this regulation that it will actually have a positive impact on the revenue to the state budget and local government units, in such a way that, as we estimate, annual costs of introducing this reduced rate will amount to 270 million and the benefits of the total of PLN 277 million from this regulation. What will the changes sealing the income tax system consist of? The Act provides for a uniform definition in both tax laws of the conditions for recognising non-residents' income as being achieved in Poland, the definition of new rules for determining the income on the holding, shares in the company in exchange for a non-monetary contribution, the conditions for using preferential tax treatment in the case of mergers and divisions of companies, the conditions for applying the exemption from flat-rate taxation at the source of interest and royalties, the rules for determining the cost of obtaining income in the event of the sale of shares, the shares of the acquiring company or the newly established company. Ladies and gentlemen, I would also like, because such questions have often been raised, to recall certain statistics. At the moment, as far as small CIT taxpayers are concerned, we have a group of 393 000 entities. The number of taxpayers who have shown the tax due in this group is 135 000, the number of other taxpayers - 258 000, including the number of taxpayers who have suffered losses, showed a lack of income for tax, i.e. 115 000, the number of taxpayers who have achieved only tax-free income is 114 000, and the number of taxpayers who have deducted losses from previous years is 14 000. This slightly reflects the scale and extent of who and to what extent can be the beneficiary of this regulation. One senator asked whether the consolidating environments were represented in the committee during the work. I can say this: in the Senate committee maybe no longer, but in earlier stages they were represented. It has been requested to join the group of regulated entities with higher revenues; there has been an attempt to add to the privilege of entities with higher revenues than EUR 1.2 million. There was a proposal for EUR 10 million, but the tax impact on the lost revenue of the state budget would be much greater in this case, over PLN 1 billion. We believe that at this stage there is no delegation for such an extension. It is not excluded that we will be returning to this topic in the future, but now we recognise that this scope of the bill is fulfilling the postulate declared during the electoral campaign and gives the opportunity for small companies to become bigger, medium-sized and medium-sized companies to become big players. There have also often been questions as to why such a privilege cannot be enjoyed by entities that do not operate in the form of commercial law companies. There's one thing worth realizing here. In those companies which are regulated, dividends are also taxed and dividend payments are taxed once again. There is therefore no simple comparison with personal income tax and such a large imbalance as it seems at first glance and in the first basic assessment to someone who forgets this basic fact. ParlaMint-PL_2016-08-03-senat-24-1.u94	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! First of all, I would like to say that the main motive that dominated our committee's meeting was that this law, which had previously been passed in the Sejm, was adopted, which is rare in this term of office, in an atmosphere of very large consensus and cooperation between the opposition and the coalition. This has been stressed several times at the meeting of our committee yesterday, and this is why our committee has adopted this proposal unanimously. I recommend to the High House, according to the report, that it be adopted without amendments. This project deals with a number of issues. The first issue is the enlargement of the National Consultative Council on Disabilities, which operates with a government representative for people with disabilities with a large number and greater representation of NGOs. The term of office of the previous Council ended in October. Non-governmental organisations have been found to be represented in this Council for two terms. Representatives of both central and local authorities and trade unions were also maintained in this representation. The second question was to change the requirements regarding the qualification of candidates for the head of the Polish Migowa Language Council. It was found that such a candidate for the presidency of the Council must meet the following requirement: know Polish sign language and Polish Not as it has been so far, when there was a requirement to speak three languages that are spoken by the deaf. Now it will be welcome, but it won't be necessary. Another issue is the shift to 1 August 2017 of the introduction of a new model for the card of a disabled person. Finally, the last two things are a matter of detail. This has so far been the case, and the administrative courts have pointed out that only in a situation where sheltered work facilities have misusing the funds collected in the company's rehabilitation fund, such misuse was subject to appropriate sanctions, but it was not subject to appropriate sanctions to the mere failure to set up such a fund. At the moment, according to the project presented to you, such non-creation of an occupational rehabilitation fund in a protected work establishment will also be subject to appropriate sanctions. Finally, the last issue that the Act deals with is the possibility for companies to apply protected labour for 10 years to pay back arrears which are created in terms of financing social security or other public-law obligations. So much for my report. As I have already said, as a result of the vote at the committee meeting, I recommend that the High House adopt a law without amendments. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-08-03-senat-24-1.u151	Mr. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen of the Senators! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Committee on Family, Senior and Social Policy a report on the meeting which took place on 2 August 2016 on the Act amending the Act on Family Support and Replacement Care, the Act on Social Assistance and the Act amending the Act on Municipal Government and some other laws. This is print number 256. The Act was adopted by the Sejm on 22 July 2016. It constituted a parliamentary submission; Parliamentary printing No 679. Work on the bill was carried out by the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs, including the Subcommittee on Family Affairs. The Commission has amended the draft law. The proposed changes to the rules on the functioning of day support facilities and the draft law in legislative terms have been discontinued in the draft. The rapporteur did not attend the meeting of the Senate Committee on Family, Labour and Social Policy. The law adopted by the Sejm aims, first, to change the way in which the organisation of joint economic and administrative service and organizational care facilities is organised, second, to make changes in the merger of different organisational units of local government. According to the justification for the bill and the explanations present at the meeting of representatives of the Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Policy, including Minister Bartosz Marczuk, this is often dictated by the occurrence of negative phenomena threatening the well-being of minors in nursing institutions. The Act introduces the principle that, in any case, provision of joint service to day-care institutions or nursing establishments shall be managed by the head of the body which provides this service by means of the daily educator appointed at the various facilities. The provision on the possibility of combining forms of institutional care with social assistance organizational units by the county or local government will not apply to care institutions of the family type. The Act bans the location of care facilities on one ground property and in one building with social assistance agencies, educational system organisational units, support centres for victims of violence in the family, medicinal entities and judicial agencies or a sobering chamber. It also limits the possibility of housing social assistance units in one building with a facility providing 24-hour care for disabled persons, chronically ill or elderly persons and organisational units of other systems. The Act sets out the possibility of combining certain organisational units of social assistance. Thus, at the level of the municipality, the social assistance centre can be combined with the support centre, but excluding the support centre for people with mental disorders, as well as the social assistance centre for the elderly with the support centre for the elderly. The county will be able to connect the district family support centre with the crisis intervention centre. The Act also amends the transitional provisions so that administrative centres for day-support and institutional replacement services operating before 1 January 2016, i.e. before the date of entry into force of the Act amending the Local Government Act and certain other laws, can continue to operate on the basis of the provisions in force prior to that date, and not only for a period of 12 months, as is apparent from that Act. It extends the period for the adjustment of family support and foster care units and social assistance units to 31 December 2019. The Legislative Office stated that the Act did not raise any substantive legislative objections, but it submitted a proposal for an amendment resulting from the legislative technique used. This amendment received the approval of the Minister of Family, Labour and Social Policy, was taken over by the senator and then voted positively by the senators. Two more amendments were tabled during the committee meeting, which eliminate the inconsistencies concerning the connection of care facilities with the 24-hour social assistance units, and add specialist support centres to this list for victims of domestic violence, in line with the systematics adopted in the law on anti-violence in the family. The discussion at the committee meeting focused on the validity of the ban on the organisation of care facilities on 1 plot and in 1 building with the various institutions listed in the Act. These amendments have been welcomed by the Minister for Family, Labour and Social Policy. In the vote they received approval from most senators. Five senators voted in favour of the bill and one senator abstained. Thank you for your attention.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-08-03-senat-24-1.u152	Thank you very much. I ask the rapporteur of the Committee on Territorial Government and State Administration, Senator Arkadiusz Grabowski, to submit a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-08-03-senat-24-1.u153	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Since my previous speaker discussed the content of the amendment of the law quite thoroughly, I, as rapporteur of the Committee on Territorial Government and State Administration, will try to make this brief. It is a law prepared on the basis of a parliamentary project, it was proceded in the committee of the local government. The revision of the law aims to eliminate adverse solutions to the functioning of family support units, foster care systems and social assistance. The amendment of the law prohibits the placement of care facilities, as my previous speaker said, on 1 real estate and in 1 building with, among others, sobering rooms, social assistance centres, law enforcement agencies, youth education centres and support centres for victims of violence in the family, hospices, care and medicine facilities. The main purpose of the bill presented by Minister Marczuk is to fear that there will be negative developments threatening the nature of care and thus endangering the safety of children there and the functioning of social assistance organisations. As far as the catalogue of these bans is concerned, it is in 1 of the articles, as I recall, in the article. Article 106 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 3a. This is a directory indicating 11 types of units that cannot be combined. The Act also departs from providing support to the family and the foster care system for joint administrative and financial and organisational services. ParlaMint-PL_2016-08-03-senat-24-1.u175	Thank you very much. Dear Marshal, Dear Ladies and Gentlemen, Senators, I would like to present a report on the activities of the Supreme Court in 2015. The activities of the Supreme Court are limited by the provisions of the Supreme Court Act and the Constitution. The Supreme Court is the judicial authority appointed to exercise justice by ensuring, in the context of supervision, the legality and uniformity of the case law of universal and military courts. But this exercise of justice and supervision only concerns matters of Judaism, not administrative issues, and therefore the Supreme Court and the first President of the Supreme Court are not responsible for the activities of universal courts in the field of extra-dudication. I stress this at the moment because during the session in the Sejm there were accusations that I did not have control over the general administration of the judiciary. Right away, in advance, I say I can control this if you grant me this authority. So we recognize cassation complaints, cassations, remedies, resolutions determining legal issues, and beyond what is very important, electoral protests, the validity of elections to the Sejm and Senate, the election of the President, the validity of the national referendum, the constitutional referendum, we also accept complaints against decisions of election committees on the adoption or not of financial statements, which is practically very important. In addition, the Supreme Court gives its opinion on the laws and other normative acts on the basis of which the courts adjudicate and function, and other laws in so far as it considers it necessary, on purpose, in connection with the judicial system. Therefore, we do not give an opinion on all legal acts, considering that only those that concern the justice system or some constitutional values that need to be taken into account in the act are important to us. Each of these activities of the Supreme Court poses different challenges to the Supreme Court and represents a separate perspective of evaluation and its activities. Of course, the primary task of the Supreme Court is the judicial function. In this context, there was a slight increase in the number of cases in 2015 But I would like to point out that this was not a big increase. The largest number of cassation complaints were brought to the Civil Chamber, then to the Chamber of Work, then to the Criminal Chamber. Only 12 were brought into the military because there's a limited scope of action here. A total of 173 legal issues were raised, 1 thousand 507 complaints. Other cases include appeals against decisions of the Military District Courts, actions for the annulment of a final decision, competition and consumer cases, actions for the length of proceedings, actions for the reopening of proceedings, requests for referral to another court, appeals for a pardon and, which is likely to be important for the senators, disciplinary matters, including judges. In the latter group of disciplinary matters concerning disciplinary liability of judges, the Supreme Court ruled 82 cases. In 30 cases, the decisions of the previous instance were not upheld, 17 were repealed and retrialed, 9 cases were changed, 1 was repealed and surrendered, 3 were repealed and the penalty was changed. I will also tell you what was interesting in the Seym: the judge was removed from office in the first case. As for lawyers, lawyers, notaries, prosecutors, it was only 76 cases. So the most important thing was the judge's case as second instance. In total, the Supreme Court recognized 10,000 502 cases. In accordance with the 2015 Presidential Regulation on determining the number of judges in the Supreme Court and the number of governors, the number of judges in the Supreme Court has been defined as 93 persons. The cast at 31 December was 84 people. On a periodic basis, the Supreme Court has benefited from the assistance of 9 judges sent by the Minister of Justice at the request of the first President of the Supreme Court. The most in the Criminal Chamber, the least in the Civil Chamber and social security. In the penal chamber, the judges of the military chamber also ruled in rotation, because there is quite a small bet. In the Supreme Court there are judges of common courts, appointed to the Supreme Court by common courts, that is, the majority, 60 persons. There are only 8 military judges in this year's term of office. The scientific community is represented 14 people And a lawyer, by 1 person. So the common judges are 60 at the moment. When I mention the number of judges, I would like to explain why this number of 93 judges has not been filled, even though there are some arrears in the Supreme Court. Filling this number is not that simple and easy, and it is not very fast. And if we want to accept common court judges to the Supreme Court, then we also need to see their merits, but in the Supreme Court And this is a different judgment than in common courts, because it is a ruling more on legal matters than on cassians. I would like to stress that the complexity of the law and the interdisciplinary nature of the problems make this ruling not a simple ruling and increasingly the Supreme Court refers to European international law, because it must do so, and the case law of the European Court of Human Rights or of the Court of Justice of the European Union. The largest Chamber, the Civil Chamber, is, of course, ruling to the greatest extent, and the subject matter is very extensive, just as the entire civil law is extensive. Competition is only in the work room, it's a little civilistic, and everything else is in the Civil House, from commercial law to copyright. This is a complex system and an interdisciplinary nature of this case law. Here I would like to recall, just as an example of interdisciplinaryness, which occurs and creates difficulties, all those judgments which concern Warsaw real estate. Because here, for example, the limitation of claims is settled, but also on the basis of the provisions of K.P.A., which no longer applies, art. 160 c.p.a. Resolution III of the CZP 78/14 removed doubts regarding the finality of the supervisory decision, because later this statute of limitations is valid for this supervisory decision and the beginning of the limitation period is related to K.p.a. Similar links with administrative law are shown by other resolutions made in enlarged warehouses, concerning liability for damages, as well as concerning the decree on ownership and use of land in the capital city of Warsaw, because there is also a supervisory decision and we will count on it and the moment of limitation, and damage. In the case law of the Civilian Chamber of the Supreme Court, the balance of constitutional values resulting from the Article is constantly supported for legal trading. 2 and Article 2 20 constitutions, i.e. the social market economy and social justice. These aspects concern in particular Resolution III of the CZP 73/15 relating to shelves charges, i.e. other than the margin of benefits for the acceptance of goods for sale. The need to balance social and individual interests has been apparent for a number of years in the case law on serviceability. Last year, I was talking about the fact that the lack of inter-temporal regulations caused... the Supreme Court faced the need to fill the legal gap by taking an ad casum of interpretation of the law. This, of course, does not meet with our approval, but we take on our own shoulders this caselaw and bring to some broad water institutions of service transmission. The law of trial is extremely important and the most judgments of the Civil Chamber are within civil procedural law. It goes on, among other things. about statements concerning the jurisdiction of the courts and local courts; this is a clarification and exclusion of certain irregularities. 7 cassation cases identified in 2015 concerned the compensation of the insurer to insured persons for the activities of insurance agents. That means art. The 11th Act on Insurance Pricing seems to be a little out of line, since there are so many cases that go up to the Supreme Court. The Civil Chamber has asked questions for a preliminary ruling to the European institutions twice. The point is to draw your attention to this, because that means that this compatibility of national law with European Union law is not always sufficiently examined in Parliament. In the case law of the Criminal Chamber, matters of substantive and procedural law were dominated. Other issues were related to EU law. As regards the procedure, in case I KZP 21/14, this is a resolution of the full composition of the Criminal Chamber, which is why I am talking about it. It has been accepted that in cassation proceedings it is permissible to set aside the contested decision only in the part in which it does not contain a decision on the criminal measure. A particularly important issue from the point of view of the defendant's procedural interests was the resolution I KZP 4/15, which stated that the provision of the article. 377 k.p.k. is a stand-alone basis for adjourned hearing still in a situation where the defendant, who has not yet made an explanation to the court, has been informed that he will be heard and of the earlier date of the hearing, has been notified of the new date of the hearing in any lawful manner. The resolution is important from among procedural threads Penalty Chambers for polygraphs, that is, polygraphs. The Supreme Court's decision on slot games was also important. The proceedings were suspended here in 2015, because the Court of Justice is issuing judgments in this case and it is necessary to wait for them. Many of the resolutions of the Supreme Court Criminal Chamber concern specific provisions of the Criminal Code. With such an interesting judgment... Such an interesting judgment is the case I KZP 5/15, in which it was found that the subject of the offence referred to in Article. 299 of the Labour Code may be held in a bank account with cash up to an amount equal to the value of the property benefit. In case I KZP 7/15, it was found that the deliberate deprivation of liberty of another person under special conditions may be regarded as a crime against humanity, the criminality of which is not subject to statute, even if it does not comply with the marks of a prohibited act referred to in Article. 118a §2 para. 2 k.p.k. There have been 2 resolutions regarding the amendment of k.p.k., but this case is already overtaken, because we already have another k.p.k., so they should not be taken into account in practice, they will not be taken into account. The judgment concerning the article is also important. 302 §1 k.k., in the insolvency case. The lack of satisfaction of even one creditor is already sufficient to attribute the mark of that crime. The Supreme Court also held that the final order of the court to maintain the order of the prosecutor to terminate the in-rem investigation was a judgment terminating the proceedings, i.e. it may be subject to an emergency cassation. I will now go to the Chamber of Labour, Social Security and Public Affairs. Most of the cases concerned social security. Here the value of the subject matter of appeal is not very strongly defined. The value of the challenge in general is low in labour and insurance matters, which is why there is a great deal of it. This, of course, is a matter of Parliament, whether it will increase the value of the challenge or not. A constant request from the Supreme Court is a certain increase in the value of the subject matter of appeal, since minimum and average wages are being increased, but this, of course, belongs to Parliament. The consequence of non-raise is the increasing influx of cases that are suitable for resolution by means of a cassation complaint. As far as the Chamber of Labour is concerned, first of all it is important to draw attention to the resolution of the enlarged Supreme Court, which is very important for working members of the board of directors of capital companies. It stated that a member of the board of directors of a public limited liability company is entitled to social insurance, but not only to a public limited liability company, which has concluded a contract with that company for the provision of management services in the context of its non-agricultural activities, is a contract for the provision of services, i.e. III PHP 2/15, not economic activity. The second issue that was important for the practice of applying insurance law in the reporting year 2015 also referred to the subject of social insurance and related to agricultural insurance. The Supreme Court took the view that there was no individual instruction by the pension authority on the obligations arising from the Act on Social Insurance for Individual Farmers, i.e. the obligation to submit within the time limit a certificate from the competent tax authority of the amount of tax on income from non-agricultural economic activities does not result in the repeal referred to in Article. Article 5a (1) (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 is replaced by the following: The 6th provision of this Act, and this repeal, is the cessation of social security. So these people don't lose their social insurance. Only because they didn't notify the income because they weren't instructed. With regard to labour law issues, it is worth paying attention to a resolution which settled doubts about the reimbursement of costs to the General Court for commuting. The Supreme Court has expressed its view that a court judge who has permission to reside outside the court and to use a private passenger car to travel from his place of residence to the court is not entitled to reimbursement of travel expenses above the amount determined by the employer, which is the intention of the Ministry of Justice. It was also important to resolve the doubts of the court, which raised the question of whether an employer who employs at least 20 employees is subject to the exemption procedure contained in the Law on the so-called. Group redundancies or not. The Supreme Court considered that the case required clarification by the Court of Justice of the European Union What is a group exemption or a definitive... whether it is sufficient to give a change of name and therefore refer the case back to the general court to ask the appropriate question. The 2015 presidential, parliamentary, referendum and supplementary elections to the Senate have made the involvement of the Chamber of Labour, Social Insurance and Public Affairs very large in public affairs, and this has affected the emergence of greater arrears in this House in the resolution of cases, let's call it ordinary. This is a result of the fact that the electoral code and the referendum law set specific deadlines for the Supreme Court, and the Supreme Court cannot fail to comply with these deadlines, i.e. it must postpone all other cases, apologise for colloquialism, aside and rule only on the validity of elections and referendums, etc. So much for discussing the most important decisions of each Chamber, I believe. It is not a monocentric system, as it was once when the national legislature issued laws and it was the only legal order. Now it is a very complex system based on international law, including the law resulting from our membership of the European Union, on national law and finally, it must be said, on a kind of precedent established by the case law of the Court of Human Rights in Strasbourg, which follows the European Convention on Human Rights, which contains only a dozen substantive provisions governing certain rights and which are the subject of the case law of the Strasbourg Court, which only gives them concrete content. And praise is given to the Supreme Court for the fact that in its case law it often refers to the case law of the European Court of Human Rights in Strasbourg, taking the enormous effort to interpret our law in the light of these standards, whose failure by us often results in Poland losing its cases before the European Court of Human Rights in Strasbourg, especially as part of a pilot system for examining cases when it is established that our law is dysfunctional and sometimes even the judicial system. I would like to recall this, for example, in the context of the recent judgment of the European Court of Human Rights in Strasbourg in the Rutkowski case and others, in which it was stated that the length of the procedure in Poland was about law and practice, which is a systemic disadvantage, which requires legislative interference and a change of practice. And it is good that the Supreme Court, at least in its case law, is trying to remedy such a situation, often referring, as I say, to these judgments. What I would like to add is that the Supreme Court also serves as a function... aimed not only at establishing a proper, but also a coherent interpretation, i.e. by the power of its authority, to force uniform application of the law. The courts of the lower courts, although formally not related, because each judge is independent, often refer to the interpretation of the Supreme Court and apply the law as the Supreme Court does. Thus, the law is applied uniformly by courts in different parts of the country. The text of the Act is contained in print No 248 and the committee's report In print No 248 A. I ask the rapporteur of the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development and the Committee on the Environment, Senator Jadwiga Rotnicka, to submit a report to the committee. You're welcome, Senator.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-08-03-senat-24-1.u233	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I report on the Act on Access to and sharing the benefits of genetic resources on behalf of 2 committees: the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development and the Committee on the Environment. This law comes from the government's submission and was adopted by the Sejm almost unanimously. It aims to introduce into the national legal system the provisions contained in Regulation No 511/2014 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 April 2014. This Regulation is a consequence of the Nagoya Protocol's requirements for access to genetic resources and the fair and fair sharing of the benefits of the use of these resources. Of course, the Act clarifies concepts such as genetic resources, like, say, all such elements that are included in this Act. The Nagoya Protocol, which was created to clarify the Convention on Biological Diversity, has created an international system that provides countries providing genetic resources with financial benefits and not only financial benefits from the new use of these resources. It also prevents enrichment practices at the expense of countries with high biodiversity. The Convention on Biological Diversity shall apply to all genetic resources: plant, animal, cellular, with the exception of genetic resources derived from humans, human. Nor is it applicable to food, which is also important. What exactly does this law contain? It shall specify in particular the characteristics and tasks of public administrations in implementing the provisions of Regulation No 511/2014 of the European Parliament and of the Council. This national public administration body is a minister, which is the minister of the environment, so I would say. The Act sets out the principles and modalities for carrying out checks on the compatibility of access and use of genetic resources and traditional knowledge related to genetic resources referred to in that Regulation, acquired outside the Republic of Poland and the sharing of benefits from their use. Finally, it lays down the rules on liability for infringement of the provisions of the Regulations which the Protocol contains. These are the most important, sort of general principles. But this law also indicates the body controlling all the records that should be included in our national law. This control body is the Chief Inspector for Environmental Protection, who categorizes part of his powers in the voivodship's environmental inspectors. When entities wishing to use genetic resources do not comply with certain provisions of this protocol, as well as our law, then... Well, the law also defines criminal sanctions, financial sanctions, in a rather large fork-break, let's say, from 1 thousand PLN to up to 50 thousand PLN, depending on how I say so, offences, omissions, etc. But it is clear that in the event of administrative penalties being imposed, the controlling authority may refrain from imposing a penalty in a situation where the entity has not actually been guilty or may not have known that it has failed to comply with something there. Finally, ladies and gentlemen, this law sets maximum limits for the expenditure of the state budget for a period of almost 20 years, 2016.2025, which is the financial result of the law, and this financial dimension is set at nearly PLN 14.73 million. This is roughly evenly divided into individual years, except in 2016, when this programme will not be fully run or financed. Ladies and gentlemen, the bill comes into force after 14 days. At the joint meeting of both committees, namely the Committee on Agriculture, I would like to say briefly, and the Committee on the Environment, which took place on 27 July, the committees took note of the programme, also listened to the positions of the government and held a brief discussion, especially with regard to financial penalties. After the discussion had been completed, the committees unanimously accepted the proposal to submit this law without amendments and asked the High House to adopt this resolution. Thank you very much.

The law therefore lays down the characteristics and tasks of public administrations in implementing the provisions of that Regulation, as well as the principles and modalities for carrying out checks on the compatibility of access and use of genetic resources and traditional knowledge associated with these resources; it lays down the rules on liability for infringements of the provisions of that Regulation; it sets out the arrangements for entering the collection of genetic resources in the Union register of collections, as required by Regulation No 511; it establishes the control body for collections already included in that register; it lays down rules on effective, proportionate and dissuasive sanctions, introducing administrative fines for infringements of Regulation No 511. We proceed with the examination of item 12 of the agenda: the Law on amending the Law on Aviation Law. The text of the Act is contained in print No 259 and the committee report . in print 259 A. I would like to ask the rapporteur of the Committee on Infrastructure, Senator Wiesław Dobkowski, to submit a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-08-03-senat-24-1.u294	Mr. Marshal! High Senate! I have the honour to present, on behalf of the Committee on Infrastructure, the position of the Committee on Infrastructure on the Law amending the Law on Aviation Law; Senate Prints No 259 and 259 A. The Act was adopted by the Sejm of the Republic of Poland on 22 July 2016. It was a parliamentary project supported by the government of the Republic of Poland. The purpose of this amendment is to clarify the rules on the organisation of the State Commission for the Investigation of Aviation Accidents and to ensure that they comply with European Union law. In addition, the Act simplifies procedures for the opening of airports of exclusive use for public use. New solutions introduced by this law... It introduces the principle of appointing the chairman of the committee and its alternates and the members of the committee for a period of 4 years by the Minister responsible for transport. The committee will include experts in the field of aviation law, air training, air traffic and air operations, as well as engineers in the field of aviation designers. The Act sets out how all the members of that committee are to be appointed. Thus, the chairman of the committee will be appointed within 14 days of the date of entry into force of this Act, while the employment relationship with the previous chairman of the committee will expire. The new chairman of the committee will request the Minister responsible for transport to appoint the deputy chairman of the committee and the secretary of the committee. The Minister shall appoint the President of the person for these posts within 30 days of the date of entry into force of this Act; at the same time, the employment relationship with the former Deputy Chairman and Secretary of the Commission will expire. The relationship of work with the former members of the committee will expire 60 days after the date of entry into force of this Act if they are not appointed as new members of the committee before that date. Those who lose their employment will be entitled to a three-month pay check. The second issue governed by this law, by this amendment, is the changes concerning the temporary opening of an airport of exclusive use for public use for a particular category of aircraft and at certain times of the day. After the adoption of this Act, the aerodrome of exclusive use may be opened to all aircraft within the time limits and hours laid down by the airport manager and made public. A new category of airports is introduced: they will be non-certified public service airports. Such a change in airport availability will be made by appropriate registration in the airport register at the request of the airport manager. From these airports only domestic flights for charter operations carried out exclusively by helicopters and aeroplanes with a maximum take-off mass of less than 10 t or with a number of passenger seats of less than 20 shall be available. Local flights and flights other than those previously mentioned other than commercial flights may also be operated. The bill's about safety. There will be fewer of these permits, red tape, just to turn the airport into a universal airport, provided, however, that it does not impair the safety of the flight and the safety of the landing. Technical and operational requirements for non-certified airports are expected to be similar to those for exclusive use airports. The Act will enter into force 14 days after the date of publication, except for the provision authorising the Minister for Transport to determine by regulation the circumstances and the way in which the flight manager is designated, the requirements which must be met by the flight manager and his powers and duties, the scope of documentation and the technical means necessary to ensure that the flights are operated. This provision shall enter into force 3 months after the date of publication. Mr. Marshal, High Senate, the Committee on Infrastructure is proposing to the High Senate that the bill be adopted without amendment. Thank you.

We proceed with the examination of item 13 of the agenda: the Law amending the Law on the provision of health care financed by public funds and certain other laws. The texts of the Act are contained in print No 250 and the report of the committee In print No 250 A. I would like to ask the rapporteur of the Committee on Territorial Government and State Administration and the Committee on Health, Senator Bogusława Orzechowska, to submit a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-08-03-senat-24-1.u338	At the meeting of the Joint Committee on Health and the Committee on Territorial Self-Government and State Administration, the law on amending the Law on the provision of public-funded health care and certain other laws was considered. The purpose and subject of this bill... The aim of the revision is to introduce regulations to ensure a transparent process of issuing opinions on the purpose of investment, which will be crucial for the development of health protection. The legislator aims to ensure that opinions are issued on the basis of reasonable grounds and thus clearly respond to existing health needs. As a result, the proposed rules are intended to ensure that the local oversupply of health services is avoided by creating inadequate resources in relation to the needs of recipients in the area concerned. The basic amendments were adopted by the Sejm on 21 July 2016 and are contained in the Act of 27 August 2004 on public-funded healthcare provision. The legislator prefers opinions on the purpose of investment in the voivodship to take into account maps of health needs of regional or nationwide maps, depending on medical entities, priorities for regional health policy and data from the register of entities performing medical activities. In order to give an opinion on the purpose of the investment by the voivodship, the law also takes the need to consult the director of the voivodship branch of the NFZ, and when it is the responsibility of the Minister responsible for Health to issue an opinion on the purpose of the investment, it is necessary to consult the President of the NFZ. It also introduces a deadline for setting priorities for regional health policy. The previous law stated that the opinion was issued in the form of an administrative decision. The current amendment amends the way in which an opinion is issued on the purpose of the investment, both in the part concerning the procedure and the reasons for the opinion. At the same time, the Act excludes the obligation to issue opinions on the purpose of certain individuals, such as a rescue unit or a trauma centre. The amendment introduces strict rules on the request for an opinion. One of the requirements set out in the Act becomes the completion of the form of the Investment Proposal Assessment Instrument in the Health Sector, IOWISZ, which is an important tool in this procedure. A scoring system shall be used to assess the purpose of the investment in the IOWISZ form. Each entity involved in the procedure with regard to its jurisdiction shall complete this form by awarding points for the fulfilment of the individual criteria. Applications are to be dealt with in the order of impact. An opinion on the appropriateness of the investment will be issued within 45 days of the date on which the application is submitted. The amendments introduced in the Act of 28 April 2011 on the Health Information System are a consequence of the provisions adopted in the Law on the provision of public-funded health care, the introduction of IOWISZ as a field ICT system. The law shall enter into force on the day following that of its publication. There have been a number of discussions, and at the end of the discussion, a proposal was made to adopt the law without amendments, 14 senators voted in favour, 6 abstentions.

The word "translation" has a completely different meaning for us. Those who follow the text see that the word "translations" has been replaced by the word "works." The discussion has been going on for years. I've been in it before. ParlaMint-PL_2016-08-03-senat-24-1.u391	Dear Marshal! High Senate! I would like to briefly present the position of the government. This is, of course, an exception that is detrimental to collecting societies. By default, it can be said that if it is unfavorable for collecting societies because they do not exercise these rights, it is the same for creators. It's like whenever we're dealing with copyright, we have to judge between the interests of a group of creators and groups of other entities, those that, for example, are. give the works of the creators, allow the spread of these works, etc., etc. And that's the case. We are obliged to introduce this European order in Poland, i.e. to implement a directive that was not implemented. All the more so we are committed to the fact that the representatives of this second group of interests, not creators, in this case cable television, have already challenged that the directive has not been implemented in its entirety. Because the word "translation" is obviously ambiguous in different languages, in different contexts. My department, which deals with these matters, has been in relations with the Commission for a long time, because it has to deal with these matters through Brussels. This is the safest thing for the next problems with the European Commission: literal translation. The practice is also that this provision will be challenged and specific issues raised between collecting societies and cable television will be brought to court. Depending on how we present this provision, in these courts probably one or the other party will have a more favourable position. In my opinion, we should stick to this compromise and the literal translation, especially since in the directive in question, in other articles the word "works" occurs, i.e. there are pieces, and there are no "works" here. So we can presume, however, that the intention of the legislator was to define the transmission, however literally we would interpret it later. 'Cause I'm gonna agree that there's no ambiguity here. I repeat that once again with the government's argument. The point is that, firstly, in our understanding, this is a compromise, and we must always seek compromise; secondly, literal translation protects us from further problems with the European Commission. ParlaMint-PL_2016-08-03-senat-24-1.u397	Thank you, Mrs. Marshal. Dear Marshal! High Senate! On behalf of the Legislative Commission and of the Committee on Human Rights, the Rule of Law and Petitions, which held yesterday on the Act on the 14th item of today's agenda, I would like to present a report on the work of the Joint Committees. The subject matter of the work was the draft law introduced by the Council of Ministers concerning the Law amending the Law on Civil Procedure, the Law on Notariat and the Act amending the Law on Civil Code, the Law on Civil Procedure and some other laws. The purpose of the amendments is to extend the scope of the Revenue Register to the European Certificate of Succession referred to in Regulation No 650/2012 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 4 July 2012 on jurisdiction, applicable law, recognition and enforcement of decisions, acceptance and enforcement of authentic instruments relating to succession and on the establishment of a European Certificate of Succession. This Regulation indicates that the principal factor in determining jurisdiction will be the habitual residence of the heir at the time of his death, unless the heir himself indicates the law of another Member State of the European Union, but must be a national of that State. The regulation is intended to facilitate the exercise of the right to inheritance by citizens of the European Union. At national level, the regulations contained in this Regulation have been taken into account following the addition to the Code of Civil Procedure of the new Title VIIa fully devoted to the instrument of the European Certificate of Succession. On 10 July of the previous year, as a result of the amendment, the Land Register was introduced, i.e. the public register, which was entrusted to the National Council of Notaries. The register shall contain information relating to the statements of inheritance by the courts, as well as the certificates of inheritance issued by the notaries. The European Certificate of Succession will be registered in this register. This will make it easier to confirm the right to inheritance. The Legislative Office raised no objections to the draft amendment. The draft was adopted unanimously on the committee. The Commission proposes that the law be adopted without amendment. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-08-03-senat-24-1.u399	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I'll try to be brief. It seems that the project is not controversial, although it is important in terms of the completeness of our legal system and the fulfilment of the obligations arising from membership of the European Union, that is, what follows from the regulation already mentioned by Senator rapporteur, Senator Ambrozik. The main purpose of the Law on the amendment of the Civil Procedure Code, the Law on the Notariat and the Law on the amendment of the Civil Code, the Law on Civil Procedure and some other laws is to extend the scope of the register of succession to the European Certificate of Succession. As the rapporteur has already mentioned, the inheritance register in its original form could not include European certificates of inheritance, since, at the time of finalising the work on the 10 July 2015 Act, which was already mentioned here, aimed at implementing Regulation No 650/2012, including the provisions on European certificates of succession, work was still ongoing. This law is therefore intended to implement what could not be implemented at the time of the adoption of the original provisions on the register of successions and to complete the process of adopting national provisions for the implementation of Regulation No 650/2012. I would like to stress that, in the view of the government, the extension of the inheritance register to the European Certificate of Succession is absolutely desirable. First of all, registration will increase the effectiveness of European inheritance certificates and thus contribute to the effective implementation of Regulation No 650/2012. What is equally important, the inclusion of the inheritance register of European inheritance certificates will increase the credibility of the succession register as a whole. This will strengthen legal certainty and minimise the risk of issuing conflicting decisions or notarial documents confirming the right to inheritance. For these reasons, the Act contains provisions amending the provisions of the Civil Procedure Code, the Notariat Law and the Act of 10 July 2015. This will make it possible to register notarial decisions and actions concerning European certificates of succession in the succession register. Such registration shall be carried out by an official court or notary depending on who issued the document to be entered in the register. The Act contains a number of additional amendments, specifying, correcting or adjusting. The Sejm has passed a law that takes into account all relevant solutions proposed in a government project with minor legislative or editorial corrections. In addition, it has introduced 3 amendments to the amended Act of 10 July 2015, which will assist in its proper implementation and application. The government welcomes these additional changes. At yesterday's joint meeting of the Senate committees The Legislative Committee and the Committee on Human Rights, Rule of Law and Petitions They unanimously voted in favour of the law without amendment. I therefore ask the Senate to adopt the law in this form and I would like to stress that in accordance with the article. 5 The Act is to enter into force on 8 September 2016. This date is so important that the provisions of the Act of 10 July 2015 enter into force by that date, which inter alia. enter the Land Register and which is amended by this Act. If this law is adopted by the High House, on 8 September we will have a complete register of inheritance as regards both this national part of the Act of 10 July 2015. And the part we're talking about today. Thank you very much. [[The meeting is chaired by Marshal Stanisław Karczewski]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-08-03-senat-24-1.u412	Dear Marshal, Ladies and gentlemen of the Senators! High Izbo! In accordance with the rights of the Ombudsman, the Ombudsman shall uphold the rights of the child as defined in the Constitution, the Convention on the Rights of the Child and other laws, taking into account the respect and responsibility of the rights and obligations of the parents. All actions undertaken by the Ombudsman in 2015, as in previous years, aimed at ensuring the full and harmonious development of the child, were carried out with respect for its subjectivity and dignity. For years, the Ombudsman has been conducting a continuous and systematic analysis of problems affecting the child and the family, as well as measures to protect the child from violence, cruelty, exploitation, demoralisation, negligence and other mistreatments. They shall act on their own initiative and on the basis of the signals received in individual cases. The Ombudsman also conducts activities to promote and disseminate the rights of the child and the methods of protecting them. In this respect, cooperates closely with NGOs. The amendments introduced in 2015 to the Ombudsperson's Act ensured a more efficient functioning of the Ombudsman's institution, which consequently translates into even better protection of the child and his rights. It was important to give the Ombudsman of the rights of the child the right to refuse to disclose the data of the person from whom the Ombudsman was informed of the breach of the rights or interests of the child and the documentation collected during the investigation. In this way, the Ombudsman has become a body to which victims or witnesses of child harm may report without fear of disclosure of personal data. Compiled to the High House in accordance with the Article. Article 12 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Act on the Ombudsman of the Rights of the Child, Senate Printing No 141, Information on the activities of the Ombudsman of the Children's Rights for 2015, together with the Notes on the state of the child's rights, provide a detailed description of the actions and actions taken to implement the constitutional and statutory tasks in the area of the protection of the rights and the welfare of the child. A total of 49,000 674 cases were brought to the Ombudsman's Office in 2015, i.e. more than four and a half times more than in 2008, when I had the honour of taking up this office. Since 2013, the number of interventions has remained close. In 2015, the Ombudsman addressed 116 general speeches to ministers, bodies and institutions. In addition, the Ombudsman, using his statutory powers, if he considered that there was a risk to the interests of the child in the ongoing proceedings, reported and participated in judicial proceedings on the rights of the prosecutor. In 2015, the Ombudsman made use of this right 231 times. In accordance with the provisions of the Ombudsperson's Act, the information presented to the High House, as well as in previous years, covers five main areas of action for the protection of the youngest. These include the right to life and health care, the right to family education, the right to decent social conditions, the right to science and the right to protection against violence, cruelty, exploitation of demoralisation, neglect or other mistreatment. In view of the wide range of written information, please allow for synthetic take-up and identification of key issues concerning the Ombudsman's action in 2015. In the area of protection of the child's right to life and health, a total of 6 000 119 cases were reported to the Ombudsman in 2015. The Ombudsman asked the authorities and institutions to take action or to provide information on, inter alia, matters relating to: access to specialist doctors, treatment costs, caries epidemics, availability for sanatorium treatment, rehabilitation of children, protection of the life and health of the unborn child, preventive vaccination, respect for the rights of minors of patients, so-called. life windows, a basket of child guaranteed benefits, a health care organisation on the population of developmental age, the organisation and functioning of health care institutions, medical errors and claims related to the provision of health services, monitoring from birth the fate of the child and safety in playrooms and playgrounds. To this extent, the Ombudsman took action on individual matters and sent 17 general speeches. In his speeches, the Advocate-General strongly responded to the recommendation of the UN Children's Rights Committee recommending the liquidation of the so-called. windows of life. The protest expressed during the proceedings of the Committee on the Rights of the Child in Geneva and several times thereafter, including in response to the written recommendations of the Committee. In the opinion of the Ombudsman, the so-called. The windows of life are an important element in complementing the protection of the fundamental right of the child to live, so he once again took the view that the natural right to live with another right should be given priority to the right to live. The views of the Ombudsman were divided by the Minister of Family, Labour and Social Policy. By directing proposals, comments, recommendations and requests to individual bodies and institutions required to ensure the best possible implementation of the rights of the child, the Ombudsman paid attention, inter alia. on the problem of a decreasing number of children and young people receiving health care This issue has been addressed by the Ombudsman since 2010; the need to prioritise the evaluation of systemic rehabilitation of children in fixed conditions; the issue of availability, quality and efficiency of dental care for children and adolescents; the still unresolved issue of the use of solar devices by children; the issue of prevention of infectious diseases in the context of an increasing number of parents refusing child protective vaccination; too easy access to prescription-free medicinal products and containing substances that have psychoactive and addictive or otherwise adverse effects on human health through undesirable effects or interactions; the need to improve the protection of the rights of minors with the most severe injuries; the need to isolate the basket of child-guaranteeed benefits; the need to work on provisions restricting access to electronic cigarettes for minors and banning advertising of nicotine-containing products other than tobacco products; the availability of optimal treatment for children suffering from tumorous sclerosis; the need to protect the brain tumours of SEGA; the problems of children in psychiatric hospitals despite the lack of medical indications and the quality of health services for children who have been granted to children and young people who have the need to be able to live in a wide manner and for the protection of children who are not intended for the purposes of a child's. The problem of drinking alcohol and other psychoactive substances by pregnant mothers. Some of the requests made by the Ombudsman in the general speeches have been taken into account. In particular, the following amendments have been made. By a regulation amending the scope of child benefits, the age limit for the provision of preventive protection with slices was increased by the end of the eighth year of age. The Law of 24 April 2015 amending the Law on the Prevention of Drug Addiction and some other laws introduced regulations on the restriction of sales of products containing psychoactive substances. On 2 December 2015, the provisions of the revised Law on State Medical Rescue entered into force, enabling the organisation of trauma centres for children. A system for monitoring the fate of the child has been sealed, a children's health book has been introduced and the need to notify the birth of the child to the POZ midwife and, in the absence of a commitment, to provide information to the district family care centre. These issues will be developed by me in the section on the state of compliance with the rights of the child. A draft law on amending the Health Act against the consequences of the use of tobacco and tobacco products was drawn up on 9 November 1995, which takes into account the demands of the Ombudsman on children's rights in 2014. This law prohibits the making available of tobacco and electronic cigarettes to persons up to the age of eighteen, the advertising and promotion of tobacco and electronic cigarettes and the smoking of electronic cigarettes in public places. I will refer more to the demands that have been made in the following part of the speech when discussing the integral part of the information presented here, i.e. the attention to the state of compliance with the rights of the child. I will now discuss the area of protection of the right of the child to be raised in the family. I would like to stress that the protection of the right to upbringing in the biological family has been a priority of the Ombudsman's activities since the beginning. There is no doubt that the natural and best environment for the child's development is the biological family. For this reason, it should be provided with care and wise help. Putting a child in foster care is the country's furthest interference in the life of the family and causes a diametric change in the situation of the minor. It is therefore necessary to examine in detail all the circumstances of the case, taking into account the highest order of the child's well-being and interests. The application of State interference measures to parental authority leading to the taking away of a child should only take place as a last resort. The replacement bake should only be used as a temporary family aid measure if other measures have not had the desired effect or where, because of the circumstances, there is a need for immediate protection of the child. It should be raised here that the right of the family to help from the state is guaranteed by the Constitution of the Republic of Poland. It is the responsibility of the State to ensure the protection of the rights of the child. A child deprived of parental care is entitled to care and assistance from public authorities. The subjectivity of the family and the autonomy of family life are protected by constitutional standards. The right of children to special care and assistance is also enshrined in the preamble and provisions of the United Nations Convention on the Rights of the Child. As regards the category of cases relating to the right of the child to raise in the family, in 2015 they were notified to the Ombudsman of the rights of the child in a total of 24 000 851. That's almost half of all the cases that came into the Ombudsman's office in the year I'm talking about. This has been the largest group of cases reported for years. When intervening, the Ombudsman asked the authorities and institutions to take action or to provide information on matters relating, inter alia, to the exercise of the right of the child to both parents, the implementation of child's contacts with parents, the enforcement of judicial decisions, young children in the foster care system, the procedure for the hearing of minors, the protection of the rights of the child in proceedings under the Hague Convention on the Civil Aspects of International Child Abduction, the right to know the identity of the child, proceedings for the taking away of a person under parental authority or under its care, minor mothers in various types of institutions, support to a biological family experiencing difficulties in carrying out care and education functions, the observance of the rights of children placed in the foster care system, the protection of the rights of the child in the adoption procedure, the operation of day support centres, the protection of the rights of minors of aliens and the ratification of the Third Optional Protocol to the Convention on the Rights of the Child. To this extent, the child's rights spokesperson acted on individual matters, but also addressed 19 speeches of a general nature. At this point, it is necessary to indicate the Ombudsman's thematic actions in the form of general speeches in the direction of initiatives by qualified entities aimed, in particular, at ratifying the Third Optional Protocol to the Convention on the Rights of the Child. It is an initiative to equip the UN Children's Rights Committee with the power to make and deal with complaints about violations of the rights of the child. The postulate was submitted by the Polish Ombudsman to the Human Rights Council in 2009. Although Poland signed the Third Optional Protocol on 30 September 2013, it has not yet ratified it. The Ombudsman continues to call for further work to ratify the document. This will contribute to strengthening the status of the child as a rightholder and improving the functioning of national mechanisms for protecting the rights of the child. The Ombudsman also called for effective protection of the right of the child to both parents. An analysis of the complaints received by the Ombudsman shows that the isolation of a child from one of the parents is a frequently chosen procedural strategy in matters relating to the regulation of the situation of the child after the separation of the parents. The Ombudsman also pointed to the ineffectiveness of the legal instruments contained in the existing provisions of the Civil Procedure Code, the Family and Care Code, and the need to criminalise the so-called. Parental abductions. The Ombudsman also called for legislative action on the provisions of the Article. 598 Civil Procedure Code. The mechanisms contained in these provisions for the performance of child-parent contacts have proved to be ineffective. The Ombudsman has requested that work be initiated on amending the provisions of the Code of Civil Procedure and on extending the catalogue of family law cases from which a cassation complaint is pending on the basis of the Hague Convention on the Civil Aspects of the Abduction of a Child Abroad. The introduction of review of decisions by the Supreme Court is in the opinion of the Ombudsman an appropriate solution to eliminate faulty decisions in this regard and to harmonise interpretation of law and judicial practice in cases resolved under the Hague Convention. The Ombudsman also asked for legislative action to be taken to allow a DNA test to be carried out in the case of the determination or denial of fatherhood or motherhood even in the absence of the consent of the alleged parent. I pointed out the problem of putting young children in institutional custody against the provisions of the Act.
