Once again, the Ombudsman asked for legislative action concerning the hearing or questioning of children during civil proceedings in the so-called. friendly interrogation rooms similar to a minor's hearing and his protection in criminal proceedings. The Ombudsman also asked the Supreme Audit Chamber to carry out checks on the performance of the obligations imposed on the organisers of foster care by the Act on support of the family and the system of foster care and to develop a comprehensive diagnosis of problems in foster care. The Ombudsman acted on the studies and findings carried out and on the prosecutor-general's guidelines on the need to ensure the proper activity of prosecutors in cases of notification pursuant to Article. 598 §1 of the Code of Civil Procedure on Obstruction of the Forced Reception of a Person Under Parental Authority or Under Care by Hiding That Person or By Taking Other Actions to Obstruction the Enforcement of the Judgment, and Renewed its Proposal to Work on Regulation of the Search by the Police of the Apartment and Other Places in Cases for the Reception of the Child. In many individual cases, the Ombudsman initiated proceedings before the court in the framework of his statutory powers, as well as proceedings already in progress. The appropriate assistance provided to the child by care institutions should be to focus on improving the functioning of the family, which does not allow proper care for children. However, the courts have not always benefited from a full range of legal opportunities to support the family, and in these situations the Ombudsman's intervention was necessary. The Ombudsman consistently takes the view that economic and family difficulties cannot be the sole reason for placing the child outside his or her natural educational environment. In situations where the child's life or health is at risk, placing in foster care is a necessary measure, but the financial situation of the family cannot be identified as a direct threat to the child's care and care situation. It should be pointed out with determination that parents' temporary parental failure due to financial failure and family poverty cannot result in a decision to take the child away from their parents. Poverty was just one of the reasons . and not the main reason To decide whether to place the child in foster care. An audit was carried out on entrepreneurs running playrooms and announced the development by the Office of Competition and Consumer Protection of the so-called "Business Halls." good practices in the organisation of children's playrooms. In the area of the protection of the right of the child to study in 2015, a total of 8 000 307 cases were reported to the Ombudsman. The Ombudsman asked the bodies of the institutions to take action or to provide information on, inter alia, matters relating to: assessing pupils, disciplinary responsibility of teachers, taking children into and staying in nursery school, organising school work, the content of the general education curriculum, the process of recruiting children for kindergartens, staying in a child institution chronically ill, supporting children with special educational needs in kindergartens and schools; correcting educational gaps and psychological and pedagogical assistance; unequal treatment and discrimination of children; safety and hygiene in kindergarten and school; the weight of schoolchildren's torches; feeding in units of the educational system; feeding children at school; the functioning of youth educational centres; the organisation of children's rest and youth; the legitimacy of applications addressed to courts and law enforcement authorities; bringing children to schools and kindergartens, including children with disabilities; and the frequently emerging problem of sewil. To this extent, the Ombudsman acted on individual matters and addressed 25 general speeches. In his speeches, the Advocate General pointed out, inter alia, for the need to amend the provisions of the law on disciplinary responsibility of teachers; for the need to define the concept of family and children legally; for the problem of applying a digital system of assessment in Polish schools; for the organisation of education classes for family life; for the need to take action in connection with irregularities in the functioning of special educational centres; for the provision of funds for education of children with special educational needs, including children with disabilities; for the issue of conditioning children's access to pre-school education from the submission of appropriate documentation on the completion of vaccinations; for the issue of non-university qualification by psychological and pedagogical counseling of central hearing disorders as a basis for a decision on the need for special education; for the need to extend the content contained in general education to primary education to primary schools on the subject of primary education, including the provision of primary education, including education in classes of I The Ombudsman gave to the Minister of National Education, developed by the Ombudsman's Panel on the standardisation of children's stay in youth education centres and youth sociotherapy centres in the fields of education, care and education, the standards of children's and young people's residence in these centres, which in the opinion of the Ombudsman will lead to the necessary changes in the functioning of these institutions. The mission and role of educational and sociotherapeutic centres is to prepare pupils for independent, creative and responsible life in non-institutional conditions. The content of the standards presented has been taken into account in the provisions of the Regulation of the Minister for National Education of 20 November 2015 on the types and detailed rules of operation of public institutions, the conditions for the stay of children and young people in these institutions and the amount and rules of remuneration paid by parents for their children's stay in these institutions. The Regulation shall enter into force on 1 September 2016, with the exception of §15, which shall apply only from 1 January 2017. A document on standardization of children's stay in foster care was drawn up by the child's Ombudsman. Standardisation in the Ombudsman's assessment will improve respect for children's rights in care institutions. The Ombudsman asked the Minister for Labour and Social Policy to take action on the dissemination of this document. In addition, the Ombudsman proposed amendments to the Regulation on institutional foster care. The Ombudsman's actions resulted in a joint speech by the Ombudsman and the Minister for Labour and Social Policy to the elders, submitting recommendations on this issue. The team's recommendations regarding the stay of children in nursing establishments are recommended in terms of the rights of the child, the rules governing the organisation of the stay of children in institutions. The implementation of guidelines for day-to-day work with children serves the proper organisation of both the care of children in institutions and the upbringing of these children. Since 2011, the Ombudsman has repeatedly asked the Minister of National Education to amend the rules on disciplinary responsibility of teachers. The subject of disciplinary proceedings against teachers was also raised in the speeches of the Ombudsman addressed to the Sejm Committee on Education, Science and Youth and the Senate Committee on Science, Education and Sport. The Ombudsman also pointed out that there were no provisions to safeguard the child's well-being in disciplinary proceedings in previous years in the observations on the state of the child's rights, which are an integral part of the annual information submitted to the High Chamber on the activities of the Ombudsman, as well as in the recommendations addressed to the UN Committee on the Rights of the Child, contained in the Ombudsman's report on the implementation of the Committee's recommendations submitted to the Committee in July 2015. It should be noted with satisfaction that most of the Ombudsman's requests for child protection in disciplinary proceedings against teachers were included in the Act of 18 March 2016 amending the Teacher's Charter and some other laws. Other requests made by the Ombudsman in general speeches have also been taken into account. And in particular... The Minister of National Education admitted that it was appropriate to change the basis of the curriculum of the subject of family education and that the current textbooks did not correspond to the expectations of pupils and parents, and also declared the establishment of a team of experts, which will develop, among others. materials for teachers of this subject. The materials for teachers were placed on the website of the Ministry on 29 October 2015. Education curators were required to take measures to support the adaptation of the functioning of special educational centres to the provisions of the Law of 20 February 2015 amending the Law on the system of education and certain other laws. The Minister of Finance, in line with the Ombudsman's argument, asked the Minister of National Education to target the issue of the allocation and spending of resources by schools and institutions to carry out tasks related to the application of special organisations and special working methods with children and young people. At the Ministry of National Education, changes were started in the system of financing the education of pupils with special educational needs, clarifying the principles of education for this group of pupils. The provisions of the new Regulation on the types and detailed rules of operation of public institutions and of the Regulation on the conditions for organising education, education and care for children and young people with disabilities, not socially adapted and at risk of social impropriety, have become a partial safeguard for pupils with special educational needs. However, it should be pointed out that further changes are needed in this area. The Minister of National Education divided the position of the Ombudsman on the question of the legality of making the child's access to preschool education dependent on the submission of documentation on the vaccinations carried out and on the respect of the constitutional right to equal access to science and compliance with the provisions of the Education System Act. All children covered by pre-school preparation must be admitted to a public institution in accordance with the law in force in this respect. A consultant for integration and work with foreign children in the Polish school was appointed. Its task is to develop training and information materials on admission to school and teaching work with foreign children, as well as to conduct information and training meetings for school heads, teachers, students, parents and local communities. As regards the rights of the child to protect against violence, cruelty, exploitation, demoralisation, negligence and other mistreatment, in 2015, the Ombudsman took 5172 cases. The Ombudsman asked the authorities and institutions to take action or to provide information on, inter alia, matters relating to: neglect and violence in the home environment, physical, mental and sexual violence, peer violence, violence in schools, kindergartens, crèches and other forms of care, violence in educational, sociotherapeutic and rehabilitation institutions, violations of the right to privacy and abuse of children. To this end, the Ombudsman took action in individual cases and sent 13 general speeches. In his speeches, the Advocate General pointed out, inter alia, to: the need for legislative work aimed at enhancing the protection of the rights of the child by ensuring professional representation of the child in legal proceedings is so-called. a probation officer; placing children over 15 years of age under compulsory interrogation in friendly conditions in criminal proceedings; making changes to the disposal of the article. 41 §1a of the Penal Code in the direction of mandatory judgment of this measure when the victim is a minor; prohibition of taking up posts, occupations or activities related to education, treatment and care of minors... The Ombudsman also pointed out the extension of the catalogue of non-disclosure convictions and the catalogue of non-prescription offences where the child is a victim of sexual crime, as well as the amendment of the Act on Radio and Television in the area of the obligation of audiovisual media service providers on demand to store copies of publicly made available advertisements and other communications, which would be a condition for their subsequent review for compliance with the Act. The Ombudsman pointed out that the courts had problems assessing the behaviour of the criminal courts in the application of criminal penalties. The Ombudsman called for action to give minors increased protection against their use for begging. The Ombudsman raised the problem of unequal access to the Large Family Charter. I also pointed out problems related to the child care allowance allowance during the period of parental leave and raised problems regarding the performance of the school obligation by minors of foreigners. The Ombudsman also draws attention to discrimination, violations of the right of the child to both parents, protection of the right to privacy of the image of the child, lack of an appropriate system to control the designation of age categories of films displayed in cinemas. An important element of the Ombudsman's activities was the request for a detailed analysis of the proceedings initiated as a result of the application by the head of the prison in accordance with Article. 9 Act of 22 November 2013 on the treatment of persons with mental disorders that pose a risk to the life, health or sexual freedom of other persons and on the taking of actions that will best safeguard ongoing and future proceedings conducted in the manner indicated by the Act. The Ombudsman stressed the need to develop and implement systemic solutions which would require courts and judicial experts to recognise these cases before the end of the penalty for applicants. In line with the Ombudsman's requests, the Ministry of Justice collects and analyses information from the Presidents of the Appeals Courts and the District Courts on an ongoing basis, as well as appropriate action has been taken in the framework of external administrative supervision. In order to discuss the involvement of the Ombudsman in judicial and administrative proceedings, I would like to remind you that during the period covered by the information, an Ombudsman acting on the rights of the prosecutor took part in 231 legal proceedings in matters of parental authority relating to the determination of the origin of the child, in matters of adoption, relating to the demoralisation of minors, in the field of social security, in matters relating to the protection of the child's property, in matters of emptying the premises and establishing the amount of the educational part of the general subsidy, and brought an appeal to the Supreme Court. The case concerned the remission of the preliminary proceedings for actions under Article. 200 §1 of the Criminal Code to bring a minor under 15 years of age to undergo other sexual activities ....and art. 202 §2 of the Penal Code, presenting pornographic content to a minor under 15 years old. The Cassation was taken into account and the Supreme Court divided the argument of the Ombudsman. In 2015, the Ombudsman declared his participation in the proceedings by the Constitutional Tribunal and supported the constitutional complaint concerning the compatibility of the Article. The 10th Act on Family Benefits with the Constitution, and also put forward a position on the proposal of a group of Members at the 7th Sejm on the compatibility with the Constitution of certain provisions of the Anti-Family Violence Act. In the field of control on its own initiative or on the grounds of notification, the Ombudsman of the rights of the child in cooperation with the Supreme Audit Office has analysed the 33 custody files identified by NIK, in which the children of the family were removed by the decision of the courts. The examination of legal proceedings did not reveal a breach of the rights of the child. The analysis is included in the NIK report entitled "Activities of public administrations in a situation of danger of taking the child away from parents'. The Ombudsman carried out 162 on-the-spot checks on the child's rights. They included forms of foster care, youth sociotherapy center, youth educational centers, independent public health care facility, social services centre, women's hut, social services house and children's and young people's rest. The labs showed a lot of abnormalities. In each of these cases, comments and conclusions have been issued. The Ombudsman carried out an examination of the state of compliance with the rights of the child in 32 randomly selected childcare facilities from all over Poland, where children aged 0 to 6 are present. The investigation revealed irregularities concerning the placement of young children in institutional care facilities contrary to the provisions of the Family Support Act and the foster care system. A detailed description of the study is given in Annex 12 to the information presented to the High House. During the period covered by the information in the performance of his duties, the Ombudsman cooperated with the Ombudsman, with the spokesperson of the patient's rights, with the Supreme Audit Office, with the General Data Protection Officer, with the Police Headquarters, with the State Agency for the Solution of Alcohol Problems. He also took part in the work of the Interministerial Group for the European Court of Human Rights, in the work of the Round Table for Persons with Disabilities, in the work of the Interministerial Group for Combating and Preventing Trade in People. The Ombudsman also cooperated with other actors, including representatives of the world of science, NGOs, representatives of various departments, as well as practices. In 2015, the Ombudsman also cooperated with Parliament On several occasions he participated in meetings of the Sejm and the Senate, as well as in meetings of the committee, among others. education, science and youth, home affairs, local government and regional policy, public finances, social policy and family, justice and human rights, health, legislation and other committees and parliamentary subcommittees. He also participated actively in meetings of parliamentary teams, including in particular the Parliamentary Children's Affairs Team, the Parliamentary Anti-Obesity Team and the Parliamentary Deafness Team. Cooperation with the parliament concerned in particular participation in the work on the Senate draft law amending the Family and Caring Code and the Civil Procedure Code, the draft law on the Family and Caring Code, the law on the Civil Procedure Code and the law on the support of the family and foster care system, in the work on the introduction of the medical document of the child's health book. In addition, the Ombudsman actively participated in consultations on 32 draft laws, regulations and other acts. In the area of international cooperation, the Ombudsman should be identified in three areas. The first is cooperation within the European Network of Ombudsmen of the Rights of the Child, ENOC and meetings promoting the protection of the rights of the child and the institution of the Ombudsperson of the World in the form of inter alia. annual meetings of the Baltic children's rights spokespersons Last year in Vilnius A study visit of representatives of the Ukrainian Parliamentary Commissioner for Human Rights at the Office of the Ombudsman in Poland and the annual meeting of the European Network of Ombudsmen of Children's Rights, last year in Amsterdam. The second area of international cooperation of the Ombudsman is cooperation with Polish consular posts. It covered complaints of Polish citizens residing abroad, concerning the activities of institutions of foreign countries which interfered with the rights of their children. The greatest number of problems reported to the Ombudsman concerned the interference of social services in the exercise of parental authority by parents or carers and decisions given in Member States determining the whereabouts of the child differently. I would just like to remind you that when dealing with such cases, the Ombudsman has limited possibilities of action. It is not entitled to request explanations from foreign authorities or to initiate proceedings before foreign courts or other institutions and participate in these proceedings. For this reason, the Ombudsman used the help of Polish consular posts and addressed his foreign counterparts, spokespersons of children's rights associated with the European Network of Ombudsmen of the Rights of the Child. The third area is cooperation between the Ombudsman and representatives of European and international institutions and meetings in the framework of the promotion of the rights of the child in the world. They were conferences, meetings, seminars. One of the most important events was that the Ombudsman produced an alternative report on the implementation of the United Nations Children's Rights Committee's recommendations to the Geneva Committee. The alternative report is Annex 14 to the information. In 2015, the Ombudsman also implemented the statutory obligation to promote the rights of the child. He initiated events and participated in events aimed at raising awareness, as well as better understanding and understanding of the rights of the youngest citizens. These activities were carried out both among children and adults. An important element here was the cooperation with NGOs, as well as social advisors to the Ombudsman who, on behalf of the Ombudsman, participated in meetings throughout the country. A total of 58 000 children and young people attended meetings with the Ombudsman in 2015. By raising awareness of the rights of the child, the Ombudsman also met with students, including medical, legal and pedagogical universities, who will work with children in the future. He visited among others. universities in Cracow, Wrocław, Słupsk, Szczecin, Warsaw, Gdańsk, Toruń, Lublin and Włocławek. In 2015, the Ombudsman sent 15 letters of commemoration. He took the honorary patronage of 287 events, in particular those organised by NGOs, scientific institutions and public administrations. By granting patronage, the Ombudsman was guided by the criterion of the dissemination of the rights of the child and actions resulting in the improvement of the respect for the rights of the child in Poland. As in previous years, the Ombudsman's Social Advisory Council and the Committee on the Codification of Family Rights at the Ombudsman's Office continued their work, working on a new approach to family law, parental responsibility and guaranteeing the subjectivity of the child. The work of this committee is coming to an end. I think I will soon present to the High House the conclusions of her work. As in previous years, also in 2015, the Ombudsman held a competition for the best doctoral and master's thesis on the protection of children's rights. As part of the Library of the Ombudsman, 4 publications were published last year, including a very comprehensive document on the rights of the child. Documents of the United Nations. With the end of the 2014/2015 school year, the Ombudsman launched a social campaign on the day of the beginning of the summer. The campaign's task was to draw the attention of children and young people to the fact that courage is also the ability to make wise decisions, the ability to assertive attitudes, responsibility for themselves and loved ones. The campaign was directed to children from middle school age. The Ombudsman's second social campaign in 2015 was the campaign of Bita, and it's time to stop. Its aim was to publicise the problem of violence against children, to change social attitudes and, above all, to reduce the level of aggression and to approve the use of physical punishments. The social movement proposed by the Ombudsman in 2013 is also continued.React to violence against children. You have the right. Comments on the state of compliance with the rights of the child, which form an integral part of the Ombudsman's activity information for 2015, as in previous years have been divided into two parts. In the first part, the Ombudsman's demands to improve the protection of the rights of the youngest citizens are pointed out. In the second, according to the systematics resulting from the Ombudsperson's Act, I point out areas where authorities and institutions should take steps to improve the respect of children's rights in Poland. As regards the proposals implemented in 2015, the changes aimed at improving the child's right to health and life by sealing the child's fate monitoring system should first be assessed positively. This system consists of 3 elements. The first is to amend the content of the Regulation of the Minister of Health of 20 September 2012 on standards of medical procedures for the provision of perinatal care services to women during the physiological period of pregnancy, physiological birth, childbirth and newborn care. These changes prevent the loss of the youngest patients in the health care system when the child is not admitted to a medical facility to receive healthcare. Preventing unwanted negligence in childcare is the Ombudsman's request from 2008 and has been implemented. The second, similar, reported since 2012 on the medical document, the introduction of the child's health book as a state document. The third standard of medical treatment for the provision of preventive care for children and young people. Work is still underway in this area in the health department and I hope that these standards will be introduced into the law. The Ombudsman also took numerous steps to improve the availability of dental services for children and young people, in his speeches to the President of the Council of Ministers, the Minister of Health and the President of the National Health Fund, repeatedly requested systematic action to improve oral status in children and young people, and identified specific problems requiring immediate action. In this respect, the only tangible change was to implement the Ombudsman's request for free coverage of guaranteed lacing of the first molar teeth in children up to the age of eight. There is still insufficient access to dental services for children, while the number of institutions performing such treatments under general anaesthesia has been increased for children with disabilities. Currently in all voivodships at least 2 institutions secure the performance of these benefits, and in most voivodships the number of these facilities is 5 and more. A year earlier there were still voivodships, where there was not one. The issue raised by the Ombudsman in 2015 was the access of children to non-prescription medicinal products. According to the Ombudsman, preparations containing psychoactive, hormonal or addictive substances should not be available without restrictions for minors. Following the Ombudsman's action, there have been changes to the law on anti-drugs and to the pharmaceutical law, they enter into force on 1 January 2017, although further changes are necessary, in particular as regards hormonal drugs. Let me remind you that the Ombudsman of the rights of the child questioned the validity of legal admission to sell a hormonal agent without a doctor's prescription. At the time, I emphasized that the Ombudsman's consent was not to pass on responsibility for taking the medicine to the child. Since 2011, the Ombudsman has repeatedly requested that access in kindergartens, schools and educational establishments be restricted to food products containing foodstuffs which adversely affect the proper development and health of children and young people, in particular the problem of overweight and obesity. The provisions that entered into force in 2015 have met the Ombudsman's demands on nutrition standards for children and young people. The Ombudsman also took steps to better protect the right of the child to be raised by both parents. One of the most important changes made was the Ombudsman's request for a change in the family and caring code in determining parental authority in the event of divorce or separation of parents. Article 58 and Article 58 of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 should therefore be amended accordingly. 107 of the family and caring code, the dependence of the court's decisions solely on the welfare of the child and the statutory status of the child's right to be brought up by both parents. For the first time, the concept of child's right to both parents appeared in Polish legislation. Here I bow to the low High House for taking this action and bringing this initiative to an end by the Senate. In 2015, the concept of a child's right to both parents first emerged. The Act also removed from the court the obligation to settle relations with the child when they are held without conflict. The court, at the agreement of the parties, is to leave the parents of the child free to form these contacts. The Code of Civil Procedure has also been amended to harmonise the composition of the court ruling in caring matters, which will undoubtedly improve judicial proceedings. The draft legislative amendment, as I have already mentioned, was drawn up by the Committee on the Codification of Family Law, and thanks to the kindness of the two Senate committees, the Committee on Justice and Human Rights and the Committee on Family, Senior and Social Policy, the Senate has taken a legislative initiative in this area. An important change proposed by the Ombudsman was the legal regulation of the so-called issue. adoption with indication. The amendment ensured control over the adoption process, introduced additional mechanisms to safeguard the rights of the child in the adoption procedure, normalized the type of adoption that has so far been a common tool to circumvent the law and to cause the child to be treated in question. The new regulation reduces the so-called phenomenon. adoptive basement. Another very important problem sought by the Ombudsman of the rights of the child was the need to standardise the conditions of stay of children and young people staying in educational institutions, both educational and social infrastructure, but I talked about this earlier. In 2015, the Ombudsman's request for changes in the principles of intra-school evaluation was met in order to build a positive attitude between pupils and physical education classes. The new provisions of the Law on the Education System and the Regulation of the Minister of Health entered into force on 1 September 2015. A particularly important change requested by the Ombudsman since 2011 was the proper protection of the children questioned in the disciplinary proceedings of teachers. The proposals implemented concern the limitation of disciplinary misconduct, the conduct of a child's interrogation only in the absence of a teacher blamed and with the participation of a psychologist. The child's parent, legal guardian or substitute guardian may also be present during the hearing. This year's amendment of the Act. The Teacher's Charter has also taken into account many of the demands made by the Ombudsman. Fuller protection of the rights of the child during organized holidays for children and young people will serve as revised provisions of the Education System Act in 2015. One of the most important changes requested by the Ombudsman of the rights of the child is the fine for the organiser of the rest, who did not make his application to the relevant school curator. Such regulation will eliminate or significantly reduce the phenomenon of illegal leisure and will allow for the verification of the organisation of rest notified by the same organiser in the following years at the beginning of its registration. Since 2013, the Ombudsman of the rights of the child has consistently raised the lack of an integrated system of free legal assistance in his observations on the state of the child's rights. This postulate was implemented in the Law of 5 August 2015 on Free Legal Assistance and Legal Education. An important element of the Ombudsman's activities was the preparation and presentation of a report containing an assessment of compliance in Poland with the Convention on the Rights of the Child. An alternative document to the government contains information on the extent to which the Polish authorities implemented the recommendations of the Committee on Children's Rights of 4 October 2002. As I have already mentioned, the Committee's final recommendations are largely in line with the recommendations of the Ombudsman in this report. I would like to stress that much has already been done towards the implementation of the rights of the child under the Convention on the Rights of the Child. For what is still to be done, we are jointly responsible for this joint work on behalf of the children of the senators, too. High Izbo, thanking you for listening to this part of the Ombudsman's information, thanking you for what has already been done, I would also like to recall the Ombudsman's comments and requests, which need urgent attention. They are described in detail in part of the information, in the observations on the state of compliance with the rights of the child. Since it is quite extensive material, I will only allow myself to signal the most important of them by password. I trust that the government and Parliament will examine the comments submitted and the proposals and will take steps to improve the respect for the rights of the child. The right to life and health. The state of compliance with the rights of the child as regards the right to life and health requires particular attention to the 23 points that I have outlined in the information, including the quality of medical services provided to children, underestimation of paediatric and dental services for children, the need for an annual examination of each child by a pediatrician, the lack of availability of specialist doctors, the lack of medical standards in the prevention of child health from five to eighteen years of age, the quality of services and access to psychiatric care, insufficient efforts to improve the condition of children's teeth, the problem of overweight and obesity among children, the quality of maternity care, insufficient healthcare in kindergartens and schools, and further, which are described in more detail in the information. The state of compliance with the rights of the child as regards the right to family education requires special attention to be paid to the 32 points indicated in this information. These include the need for better support for biological families, the need to improve parental competence, the right of the child to be raised by both parents, the need to regulate the problem of so-called. Parental abductions, low parental awareness of the consequences of abducting a child abroad, the stability of the educational environment of children placed in foster care, the problem of young children being placed in breach of the law in institutional foster care. Currently, the Ombudsman conducts further national surveys, checks this problem; judicial monitoring of the fate of the child and his biological family; the length of court proceedings; the need to improve the functioning of the family court; the problem of restricting children living outside the family of biological contacts with loved ones plus others I have already mentioned. The state of child's rights in respect of the right to decent social conditions requires special attention to be paid to... I am here pointing to 15 points to which I would like this year.... I pay attention here, among others. for child poverty, especially in large families, the problem of homelessness among children A nationwide survey of homeless people showed that we have nearly 2 000 homeless children in Poland. I would also like to draw attention to the very low effectiveness of the enforcement of benefits, to the difficulties in enforcing maintenance services imposed by the judgments of Polish courts from parents residing outside the country, to the consequences of parental labour emigration, to the lack of safeguarding the needs of families with children, plus further issues which are described in the information widely. The right to study. The state of the child's rights in this regard requires special attention... Here I present the most urgent needs in 24 points. It goes on, among other things. to improve the quality of teacher training in practical preparation for work, the practice of assessing pupils' educational achievements In particular, it is about PE classes, music, plasticism, inappropriate supervision of directors of the work of educational institutions, the application of penalties affecting the dignity of children, feeding children in institutions, financing the needs of children with disabilities pulses further mentioned in the information. And the last point: the right to protection against violence, cruelty, exploitation, demoralization, negligence and other mistreatment. In 21 points, I point to the most urgent needs. It goes on, among other things. too high a percentage of those who approve of the use of carnal punishments against children, the need to act effectively against hate speech, the problem of being too willing to respond to violence against children, the need to tighten the penalties for violence against children The relevant document is currently being drawn up at the President's office, as proposed by the Ombudsman, is the announcement of a legislative initiative . plus further issues discussed in the information. High Izbo, I hope that this information on the activities of the Ombudsman will be helpful in further parliamentary work, which will help to guarantee even higher standards of protection of the rights and welfare of the child in Poland. ParlaMint-PL_2016-08-03-senat-24-1.u446	Dear Marshal, Ladies and gentlemen of the Senators! High Izbo! I would like to thank you very much for these words, for examining this comprehensive document, in which we wanted to present the various areas of the Ombudsman's activities as thoroughly as possible, so that the senators can have a greater insight into what this constitutional authority of state control is doing. I assure you that the Ombudsman's concern is the children and the welfare of the child, the rights of the child, but in the context of good relations with parents, good family relations. It's also written in the Convention on the Rights of the Child, and in the Ombudsperson's Act. Any action taken is very carefully analysed in order to help the child, not to disrupt his reality. Phone calls and all the contacts that take place always go in this direction. The staff of the Ombudsman's Office of Children's Rights are very high-quality specialists, people who, thanks to their education, but also huge experience, take the best possible action, seeking solutions and assistance for a particular child, but also paying attention to these particular areas. It is very deep in my heart both to my colleagues and to me personally, and I have the pleasure and the honor of running this office for the past eight years. Thank you very much for listening to me at this late hour and perseverance... [[Tonight, tonight...]] ...but, I think, children's rights are an important part of our reality, because they concern 1/3 of Polish citizens. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
